- **Checked by:** robots.py: an escort trip finishes whether its group ends all aboard, some dead or all dead; a test that a boarded refugee takes no damage.
- **Tags:** valve, npc, logic, gameplay

### A Combine gate lets its squads out one at a time, a little slower each time, and a car jammed in it keeps it open (`valve-gate-waves`)

A held Combine gate stays shut until its squad of three has gathered behind it, opens, lets them out at the attackers and shuts behind them; when all three are dead the next squad comes, each one 2 s later than the last, six squads in all. A car pushed into the gateway stops it shutting, and the way through stays open. A new kind of Combine post: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep1_c17_01a: relay_gateScene ForceSpawns ptemplate_gate_soldiers (3) and Adds to counter_gateWaves (max 6, which Kills the gate relays). rallypoint_gateSoldiers OnArrival > counter_gateAssault (max 1) > relay_openGate @3: barricade_1_door_1 open (2 s), a node link on, the gate's clip off, goalassault_gateSoldiers BeginAssault @1.5. counter_combineDead (max 3, OnDeath) Adds 2 to counter_WaveRestart, whose OutValue becomes timer_WaveRestart's RefireTime (2, 4, 6 s ...): the gate shuts, the next squad @1. trigger_car_detect StartTouch Disables relay_closeGate, EndTouch Enables it.
- **How:** Gamemode: a checkpoint gate on the edge of the Combine's ground during an uprising (docs/features/escalation.md) could release squads of 3 up to 6 times, the gap growing 2 s; a vehicle or heavy prop in the gateway blocks it shutting. Map modules: lc_gate_<name> with a rally spot about 128 inside and the gate's own clip brush.
- **Checked by:** A test: the gate never shuts on a prop in its doorway, and lets out six squads at most.
- **Tags:** valve, npc, logic, combine, gameplay

### A safehouse door has a slot: it slides open, someone looks out and asks, and the door opens on the answer (`valve-slot-door`)

The Resistance's door on the street is the plain slotted door: a knock opens the slot, a face looks out and asks, the guide gives the word, the slot shuts and the door is unbolted and swung away from the guest. If the guide is killed on the way, the slot opens and shuts and the door opens anyway: nobody is left outside. Changes how a Resistance door works: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep1_c17_02b, two doors: bld_a_frontdoor_1 and barney_door_2 (prop_door_rotating door03_slotted_left.mdl), each with a prop_dynamic door03_slot.mdl on it. bld_a_frontdoor_1_ss_1 (the guide arrives) > bld_a_lcs_1 (c17_06_password01) OnCompletion slot open @1 > bld_a_lcs_2 > bld_a_lcs_3 (c17_safehouse_password: slot close @0.5, idle @1) > bld_a_lcs_4 > bld_a_frontdoor_open_rl: Unlock, OpenAwayFrom @1; OnFullyOpen turns its node link on. outpost_leader_death_rl (the guide dead): slot open @0, close @2, idle @3, the last line @1.5, the door opens. Barney's door: raid_pre.vcd Trigger1 slot Open, Trigger2 Close, Trigger3 Unlock and OpenAwayFrom @0.7.
- **How:** Gamemode: a safehouse or the base's street door (docs/features/resistance-base.md) could be the slotted door: E from outside knocks, a rebel inside (a player, or the NPC on watch) opens the slot (prop_dynamic door03_slot.mdl with Valve's 'open' and 'close'), sees who it is and lets them in; the knock and a voice, never text. Map modules: Resistance doors use door03_slotted_left.mdl with the slot prop on it. EP2's White Forest gate (ep2_outland_10a): the lookout in the gate tower calls down to the man at the gate and the gate slides open on his line (base_gate_tower_citizen_lcs OnTrigger1 > base_gate_open_relay).
- **Checked by:** map_assets.py: both models present; review
- **Tags:** valve, doors, npc, gameplay

### Most supplies found lying about give what the finder is short of (`valve-supplies-by-need`)

In a long fight through a city, most supply boxes and loose kits are not set: each gives health, a battery or ammunition for whatever the finder lacks most, by levels set on it (health first). Fixed pickups are kept for the few places where a thing is the point. Changes what our crates and cabinets give: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** The ten EP1 City 17 maps: 143 item_dynamic_resupply loose and 120 more inside the 148 item_item_crate, against 154 fixed items: 63 in 100 of supplies adapt. 114 want health full (DesiredHealth 1); pistol ammo 1 on 72, AR2 1 on 56, SMG 1 on 48; armour 0 to 0.5; grenades 0.1 on about 45. The hospital (02a) has 21 loose and 19 in crates, 03 has 43; City 17's uprising had 39.
- **How:** Gamemode: a clinic cabinet, a Resistance cache or a supply drop could hand whoever opens it what they lack most (a medkit to the hurt, ammo for the gun in their hands), inside the owner's limits (docs/features/ammo-limits.md), instead of a fixed list.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, gameplay, props

- **See:** `valve_coast/02_coast01_dock_outpost_cut.png`

### A friendly marksman on the roofs moves to cover whichever street the player is in (`valve-rooftop-cover`)

Where a player must cross ground full of the dead, a friend with a rifle takes a roof over it; each part of the ground has its own roof spot, and as the player moves into a part the marksman moves to that part's spot. A new way our rebel NPCs could help: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep1_c17_03: 7 assault goals assault_alyx_snipeposition_01 to 07 (rally points all at z 377, nearest neighbour 304 to 600) and 7 trigger_multiples trigger_alyxSnipePosition: StartTouch Deactivates every snipe goal and Activates its own @0.1. Below, endless street wanderers: npc_template_makers of antlion (5 alive), zombine (4), zombie (2) and fast zombie (1), every 0.1 s, only unseen (CriterionVisibility 1); each OnLostPlayerLOS sets its own health to 1, so her shots kill. ep1_c17_01a: Alyx becomes an npc_sniper to cover the alley, and her nags stop when the gunner and sniper she warns of are dead (counter_sniperAndGunnerDead max 2).
- **How:** Gamemode: when players fight in an infested or contested district, a Resistance NPC marksman could take the nearest of a few roof spots to them and move as they move. Map modules: lc_overwatch_N spots on roofs 300 to 600 apart, each naming the ground below it. EP2's version (ep2_outland_06): Alyx's rifle is an npc_sniper (shootzombiesinchest 1) whose laser sweeps a target group chosen by the zone the player is in (SweepGroupRandomly, one trigger per zone), painting every 1 s (varied 0.75), every 0.25 s once she guides him; killed and respawned from a template as she moves on.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, npc, gameplay

### A broadcast loops on the city's screens and is joined midway; when the Combine see you, the screens cut to them (`valve-screens-cut-in`)

A message on the city's screens runs as a loop of short scenes that starts partway in, so whoever walks up joins it in the middle; between loops the screen is blank a few seconds. When a Combine camera finds the players, every screen near them cuts to static, then the Combine's face for a few seconds, static, and off, and the lights by them go out. Changes our screens and broadcasts: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep1_c17_01, 01a, 02b, 05: lcs_kleinercast to lcs_kleinercast13 chained OnCompletion on monitor_breencast_jumbotron4 through point_camera camera_tv_klienercast; logic_auto starts the loop at scene 5 @0.5; after scene 13 the screen shows camera_tv_blank @2 and the loop starts again @5. ep1_c17_05: pen_camera_1 (npc_combine_camera, inner 288, outer 408, invulnerable until then) OnFoundEnemy > relay_advisor_scene: monitor_room* static @0, the advisor @0.5, static @3, off @3.5, the broadcast's scenes Cancelled, sound_advisors @0.5 to 3, the hospital exit's light off with a spark. ep1_c17_01: the Combine's wall crushes the screen showing Kleiner when it falls.
- **How:** Gamemode: the Administrator's broadcasts and a Resistance hijack (docs/features/city-administration.md) could loop their recorded lines starting partway in; when a Combine camera or scanner spots a wanted player, the screens near him show static and the Combine's face for 3 s. Sound and picture, no text. Breen's broadcasts in d3_citadel_03 and 04 come through one point_camera (fov 20) aimed at npc_breen in a booth far from the play, shown on every console's func_monitor; a func_brush slate in front of that camera blanks all the screens at once: the slate tone plays (at 2 and 5 s), the slate is Disabled (at 6 s, or 0.5 s after the tone) and Breen's scene starts (at 9 s, or 0.6 s); his speeches chain OnCompletion 2 s apart.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, logic, gameplay, combine

### A Combine structure is a centred mass with an off-centre skin: plates, layers, leans and turns, and no mirror (`valve-combine-recipe`)

Build a Combine wall, gate, tower or hall side in five passes: (1) masses on a 256 bay in 128 courses, which may be centred; (2) plates 2 to 16 thick laid over them; (3) 3 to 6 layers on about a third of the wall; (4) 2 or 3 leans and turns from a short list of simple ratios; (5) the relief of each wall and the two sides of each opening made to differ. It is not random: the rhythm stays on 256, the oddness is in the skin. New Combine pieces only; ask the owner first before changing the checkpoint, depot or armoury, which stand upright and square.

- **Why:** d3_citadel_01 to 05, d3_breen_01, ep1_intro, ep1_citadel_00 to 04, styleguide_core (21 November): single detail groups are mirror-true (median 0.91 to 1.0; d3_citadel_03's shaft walls 0.93 to 0.998, d3_breen_01's pillars in mirrored pairs), but 30% of 1082 articulated Combine walls match their mirror below 0.6 (old City 17: 15% of 206), 18.1% of the Combine's visible area is off the axes (old City 17 1.4%), plates are 32 to 74% of its brushes, 3+ layers cover 17 to 67% of its wall area. Notes: _dev/reference/valve_citadel/notes/architecture_notes.md.
- **How:** A map_combine.py generator (proposed): combine_piece(kind, box, seed) lays mass, plate stacks, lean courses and turns by the recipe in the notes and returns convex solids for Vmf.detail_shapes; it throws away a seed whose walls mirror (see valve-combine-no-mirror).
- **Checked by:** proposed: map_combine.py checks its own pieces (mirror match, layer share, off-axis share) before writing
- **Tags:** valve, combine, geometry, recipe

### The Combine's skin is thin plates, butted, three to six deep on a third of the wall: a big step off the mass, then small reveals (`valve-combine-plates`)

Cover a Combine mass with plates 2, 4, 8 or 16 thick (pick 2 or 3 per piece), 32 to 64 by 64 to 256 (128 the commonest; 1024 for giant ones). Butt them (no gap on 60 to 81% of joins) and give one join in five a reveal of 8, 12, 40 or 48. By wall area: 1 layer on 36 to 61%, 2 on 10 to 29%, 3 to 6 on 17 to 67%. The first layer stands 48, 56 or 64 off the mass, each layer on it 4 or 8 more: steps are big or small, rarely between. New pieces only; ask the owner first before changing a Combine piece already built.

- **Why:** Citadel maps (21 November): plates (thinnest side 16 or less, short side 3 times that) 32 to 74% of Combine brushes, median 56%. Thickness tops: 8 (26 to 38% in d3_citadel_02, 05, ep1_citadel_00), 16 (30% d3_citadel_03), 2 (25 to 28% ep1_citadel_01, 03; 39% the Nexus d3_c17_10b). Long side 128 on 22 to 33% in HL2. Same-facing faces within 64 of depth: 3+ layers on 17 to 67% of the area in 14 of 15 maps (the core 3%); steps 64, 48, 56, 4, 8 lead in every map; coplanar joins gap 0 on 60 to 81%, then 8, 12, 40, 48, 64.
- **How:** map_combine.py plate_stack(face, layers): first layer 48 to 64 out, then 4 or 8 per layer, plates from the chosen thicknesses, widths never repeated next to each other, butted with one reveal in five.
- **Checked by:** proposed: map_lint notes a Combine wall of one plate layer longer than 512 (valve-combine-plates)
- **Tags:** valve, combine, geometry

### The Combine turns off the grid by simple ratios: 45, 1 in 2, 1 in 4, 22.5; a fifth of its surface is off the axes (`valve-combine-turns`)

Turn Combine faces in plan by 45, 26.6 (1 in 2), 14.0 (1 in 4) or 22.5 (an octagon, its corners on the grid giving 22.2 and 23.5), never a free angle; one piece uses 2 to 4 off-axis directions (a showpiece up to 12). Off-axis area: 4 to 15% for a plain hall, 15 to 35% for a showpiece, 44 to 56% for the core or a wreck; the old city round it stays square (1 to 2%). Make sloped faces from three whole-number points on the 4 to 16 grid, as HL2 did. New pieces only; ask the owner first before turning anything already built.

- **Why:** Citadel maps (21 November): 18.1% of 4.6 billion square units of visible Combine area off the axes (3.8% d3_citadel_02, 14 to 15% d3_citadel_03, 05, ep1_citadel_00, 35% ep1_citadel_01, 03, 44% styleguide_core, 56% ep1_intro); intact City 17 (d3_c17_10a, b) 1.4%, ruined (ep1_c17_00, 01) 6.5%. Turned area: 45 is 83% in d3_citadel_02 and 88% in ep1_citadel_01; 1 in 2 24 to 25% in ep1_citadel_00, d3_citadel_05 and 56% in styleguide_destroyed_citadel; 1 in 4 36% in d3_citadel_03; 22.5 (as 22.1 to 22.3 and 23.5) 53% in ep1_citadel_03 and 62% in the core. Detail groups: median 2 to 4 off-axis directions, 90th percentile 4 to 12. HL2's off-axis plane points on a 4, 8 or 16 grid (9% fractional in d3_citadel_02); EP1's mostly fractional (84% in ep1_citadel_03).
- **How:** map_combine.py turn(ratio): rotate a plate stack's plan by one of the four turns, corners snapped to the 4 grid; a piece draws its 2 to 4 turns at the start and keeps to them.
- **Checked by:** proposed: map_combine.py reports each piece's off-axis share and its distinct turn count
- **Tags:** valve, combine, geometry

### No Combine wall's relief and no passage's two sides are each other's mirror; the masses under them may be (`valve-combine-no-mirror`)

On a Combine wall with three or more relief levels, make the left and right differ: different layer counts (one side 1 or 2, the other 3 to 5), different step depths, one side leaning and the other plumb, a bay 88 to 408 deep on one side only. The two walls of a passage get different relief. Aim for a third of such walls matching their mirror below 0.6 and passage sides matching below 0.3. Single pillars, a reactor core and a formal office may stay centred. New pieces only; ask the owner first before changing a Combine piece already built.

- **Why:** Depth pictures of every wall on an 8 grid, mirrored about the best upright line (21 November): articulated Combine walls 1082, 30% below 0.6 and 43% at 0.9 or more; old City 17 walls 206, 15% and 46%; per-map medians 0.57 (d3_citadel_04) to 1.0 (the core). Passages (walls facing 64 to 640 apart): median match 0.02 (d3_citadel_05), 0.07 (d3_breen_01), 0.04 (styleguide_destroyed_citadel), 0.13 to 0.30 in nine more maps; exceptions d3_citadel_02 0.96, ep1_citadel_04 0.63, the core 1.0; City 17 streets 0.18 to 0.50. Valve's detail groups mirror-true (median 0.91 to 1.0); d3_breen_01's office pillars in mirrored pairs. Nexus picture U27.
- **How:** map_combine.py builds the two sides of an opening from different draws of the plate and layer rules and scores each wall with the depth-picture mirror test (walls.py in the study); a seed scoring 0.9 or more on an articulated wall, or 0.5 or more on a passage, is drawn again.
- **Checked by:** proposed: map_lint kind mirror notes a Combine wall of 3+ relief levels whose relief matches its mirror at 0.9 or more
- **Tags:** valve, combine, geometry

### Huge against small: one Combine join in six puts a piece against one 8 times its size, and the texture grows with the piece (`valve-combine-scale-jump`)

Set small Combine parts (plates 2 to 16 thick, 32 to 64 wide) straight against masses 8 to 32 times their size on about one join in six (the old city: one in fifteen). Texture plates near the eye at 0.25 and giant faces at 1 or 2, so the panel pattern grows with the piece. New pieces only; ask the owner first before retexturing anything built.

- **Why:** Citadel maps (21 November): touching Combine pairs whose longest sides differ 8 times or more: 15.8% of 148 thousand pairs (16 times: 5.1%); old buildings 6.5% of 24 thousand (1.6%). Combine longest sides median 128 to 512, 90th percentile 512 to 1737. Texture scale on the Combine's area: 0.25 on 39 to 81% in HL2's Citadel, 1.0 on 21 to 55%, 2.0 on 42% in d3_citadel_01 (its giant walls); City 17's old walls 0.25.
- **How:** map_combine.py: each mass gets at least one small plate or fin straight on it; texture scale by the face's longest side (under 512: 0.25; to 1024: 1; over: 2).
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, combine, geometry

### The Combine keeps a 256 rhythm and breaks it with pairs and odd ones, not with random spacing (`valve-combine-rhythm`)

Repeat Combine ribs, fins and panels on a 256 pitch (or 128), then break the run: about 2 runs in 5 stay even, 1 in 5 goes in pairs (two pieces 14 to 36 apart, a pair every 143 to 285), the rest take uneven spacings (170, 36, 510). Never scatter at random. New pieces only. Pieces already built keep their look: ask the owner first before changing them.

- **Why:** Runs of 3 or more like Combine pieces in a line (21 November): 1345 in the Citadel maps, even 40% (pitch 256 on 416 of them), near even 15%, paired 19%, free 26%; EP1's Citadel freer (51 to 73% uneven) than HL2's (27 to 40%). Old City 17, 162 runs: even 47% (pitch 128), paired 17%, free 22%. Examples: ep1_citadel_03 fins 16 by 32 spaced 32, 160, 32, 160; plates 2 thick 14, 98, 14, 490; d3_citadel_04 slabs 170, 36, 510.
- **How:** map_combine.py rib_run(length, pitch): even, paired or free by the shares above; pairs 14 to 36 apart.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, combine, geometry

### Inside an old building the Combine lays plates flush and square; where its own mass arrives it drives in at an angle (`valve-combine-meets-old`)

Two ways only. Inside an old building, Combine plates lie flush on the old walls, square to them, not cut in (or stand 14 to 16 off). Where a Combine mass meets the old city from outside, it is turned against it by 1 in 2 (27) or 45 and cut 4 to 128 into the old wall or floor. Ask the owner first before changing an old building or Combine piece already built.

- **Why:** 21 November. The Nexus (d3_c17_10a, b): 43 to 79% of Combine brushes touch old ones, 2.5 to 6% cut in, 0 to 6% turned, the rest stand 14 to 16 off. ep1_citadel_00 (the Citadel's foot in the old city): 40% of touching Combine brushes cut in (to 64 and 128), 43% turned, 65% of those by 27; ep1_citadel_03 (old concrete under the core) 67% cut in 4 to 13, 29% turned by 45; ep1_c17_00 29% cut in, 29% turned by 13. Nova Prospekt's plates flush on concrete (valve-combine-lean).
- **How:** map_combine.py takes the old wall's plane: inside, plates flush and square; outside, the mass rotated 27 or 45 and sunk 4 to 128 into the old brush (a func_detail, so the old wall keeps sealing).
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, combine, geometry

### Combine light is set into the plates: flush panels, mostly upright, cold; warm only where it fails (`valve-combine-light-panels`)

Light a Combine wall with light-panel brushes (lights/white004_nochop or white003_nochop) set flush in the plate layer, 16 to 64 thick, 64 to 192 by 140 to 1150 (small rooms 8 to 16 by 16 to 100 by 48 to 256), upright 3 to 2 over lying, coloured 147 226 240 or 122 173 188; warm 254 95 48 or 249 177 106 only where the place is failing. No flickering (the owner's rule). New pieces only. Pieces already built keep their look: ask the owner first before changing them.

- **Why:** Citadel maps (21 November): 11 to 74 light-panel brushes a map; their face flush with the plate beside it on 77 to 100%; d3_citadel_03 to 05 panels 16 to 128 thick, 64 to 457 by 128 to 1280; ep1_citadel_03 2.7 to 23 thick, 15 to 192 by 16 to 392; upright 214 to lying 135. light and light_spot colours: 147 226 240 leads, 122 173 188 in d3_citadel_04, 05; ep1_citadel_01, 02b (the core failing) 254 95 48, 249 177 106, 235 114 33. Glow sprites 1 to 92 a map.
- **How:** map_combine.py swaps one plate of a stack for a light panel of the same size and plane, with a light behind it in the panel's colour.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, combine, lighting

### The Combine's weapon check is a room that announces, holds you, charges, then takes and burns what you carry (`valve-confiscation-field`)

A Combine weapon check is a timed room, never a popup: whoever steps in is held still, a voice announces the check, machines round the room charge with a rising sound, then what is carried floats out of the hands and burns away, the room flares bright, and the way out opens. What cannot be taken (Valve's gravity gun) makes the machines spark and fail, a voice says the check failed, and the way opens anyway with a security warning. A check that takes players' things changes our contraband and checkpoint features: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** d3_citadel_03 room0 and ep1_citadel_01 (the same build): a trigger_once in the middle; at 0 trigger_vphysics_motion pins the player (gravity 2, velocity limit 8); at 2 s (1.5 in EP1) citadel.overwatch_weaponstrip (radius 1250); at 7 s strip_core1, four env_citadel_energy_core (scale 3) at the room's corners, StartCharge 4 with weapon_zapper_start and a loop; at 11 s trigger_weapon_strip Enable (killweapons No), the dropped weapons float in a second vphysics trigger (gravity 0, velocity limit 2, air density 0.5); at 13 s trigger_weapon_dissolve Enable, emitter info_targets at the four cores, the room light bright. trigger_weapon_dissolve OnChargingPhyscannon > sparks, overwatch_weaponstrip_fail at +1 s, the end relay at +4 s (strip and dissolve off, the exit wall Killed, the shield wall off, autosave), overwatch_weaponstrip_fail_security at +8 s. With nothing to fail, strip_stop does the same and the first squad comes 2 s later.
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): a Combine entry check for the Nexus or a Combine-only zone (docs/features/contraband.md, patdown-and-cuffs.md): stepping into a marked spot holds the player about 10 s, plays the Overwatch weapon-strip line and a charge sound, then takes the contraband (as a search does) with a dissolve effect on what is taken, and opens the door; told by sound and effect, no text (house rule 10). Map modules: a check-room piece with four energy-core points at the corners and an exit door the gamemode opens.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, combine, logic, sound, gameplay

- **See:** `valve_citadel/15_e01_leaning_corridor.png`

### Combine power is a thing you can carry: pull the energy ball from its socket and what it feeds goes dark (`valve-ball-socket-power`)

In the Citadel what powers a field, a lift or a bridge is an energy ball held in a socket, marked by a pair of glowing cores. Take the ball out and the thing it feeds switches off; put a ball back and it comes on again. Elsewhere dispensers hold a few balls that come back a few seconds after one is lost, and catchers count balls thrown in until enough are in. A new kind of job game or sabotage: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** d3_citadel_03: func_combine_ball_spawner with flag 8192 (power supply), ballcount 1, ballrespawntime -1 (never comes back), speed 75: ballspawner_ballgenerator1 OnLastBallGrabbed > relay_combineshieldwall3_off, OnBallReinserted > _on; ball_spawner_room3 holds wall 7; two env_citadel_energy_core (scale 1.5, one up, one down) at each socket. Dispensers: ballcount 10, respawn 4 s, speed 125 to 350, start inactive (4096), in rows 80 apart. ep1_citadel_03: catchers are trigger_multiples with filter_combineball_type (ball_trap_10 Add 1, its reset Subtracts 1): 2 balls per bridge section, 3 for the upper bridge, 6 for the core (counter_balltrap). 44 spawners in 9 maps, 17 launchers in EP1.
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): a sabotage or repair job game in the Nexus or a Combine post (docs/features/jobs-and-quests.md): carry a glowing ball (a prop) from a dispenser to a socket to power a door or field, or pull one out to drop a field; a hands-on step in place of any waiting (house rule 9). Map modules: a socket point with two core glows, wired to a named field or door. EP2's version with cables (ep2_outland_06a): physics plugs carried to sockets (trigger_multiple, physics flag 8, filtered by the plug's name) that snap them in; two right sockets per route (logic_branch_listener OnAllTrue) light a lamp on the socket, the tower's lamps, the radio and a hum; pulling a plug out (OnPhysGunPickup added at run time) turns it all off again.
- **Checked by:** map_lint 'wire': a ball socket (func_combine_ball_spawner flag 8192) wires both OnLastBallGrabbed and OnBallReinserted; review
- **Tags:** valve, combine, logic, gameplay

- **See:** `valve_citadel/06_c03_ball_room_cut.png`, `valve_citadel/21_e03_core_chamber_cut.png`

### A deadline is counted out loud by the Combine's voice, closer together near the end, while a machine climbs with it (`valve-countdown-spoken`)

When something will happen at a set moment (Breen's teleport), the time left is spoken by Overwatch at 3 minutes, 2, 1, 45 seconds, 30, 15 and 10: the calls come closer together as the end nears, and a machine everyone can see moves on a step with the late calls. No clock on the screen. Ending the threat cancels the calls still to come. Our endings and the collapse are the owner's: changing how they count down, or adding spoken calls, is ask the owner first.

- **Why:** d3_breen_01: a trigger_once fires countdown (info_teleporter_countdown) StartCountdown 190 at 12 s (the wiki: its HUD was never finished) and citadel_relay_overwatch at 19 s: citadel.overwatch_3min_to_teleport @0, 2min @57, 1min @117, 45sec @132, 30sec @147, 15sec @162, 10sec @167 (ambient_generic, radius 1250, flags 49); Train_main_lift (Breen's lift up the core) Resume @147 and @162. logic_portal_final_end: citadel_relay_overwatch CancelPending @0 and Kill @0.1, countdown StopCountdown.
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): an ending or the collapse (docs/features/endings.md, collapse.md) that comes at a set time could have the city's dispatcher voice speak the time left at 3, 2, 1 min, 45, 30, 15 and 10 s, and stop the moment it is averted; a sound, never a line of text (house rule 10). EP2's launch (ep2_outland_12a relay_launch, 28 lines from one button): warning sounds at 0 and 20 s, a voice at 5, silo doors and dust at 3, birds fly off at 3, rumble dust at 10, 20, 25, a shake timer from 3 to 36 s, the rocket at 30; the count shows on four digit panels by animated texture frames (StartAnimSequence), 30 to 0.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, combine, logic, sound, gameplay

- **See:** `valve_citadel/12_breen_portal_chamber.png`

### The Combine's masters are seen only on screens; staff work consoles under them, and the alarm comes when you are noticed (`valve-overseer-on-screens`)

The Advisor is never met in the Citadel: it is a face on the screens, cut in and out between other pictures with its hum, screens round the room coming on one by one with a click each. In the control room elites work consoles while it watches; when a player looks into the room (about half a second in sight) or a timeout runs out, the Advisor alerts, an alarm sounds and repeats, the screens go dark and the staff turn to fight. New for our Nexus or Combine quarter: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep1_citadel_01, 03, d3_breen_01: no npc_advisor; a cycler with models/Advisor.mdl in a sealed room, point_cameras fov 70 to 90, func_monitors. ep1_citadel_01 relay_advisor_monitor_fx: SetCamera between camera_advisor and camera_tv_breen 9 times from 0.5 to 3.6 s with the Advisor's ambient loop on and off in step; monitors 2 to 7 Enable at 4.3, 4.6, 4.9, 5.6, 6.2, 6.5 s with a monitor-on sound each; AdvisorAlert @3.5, AdvisorTalk @5; relay_bringoutpod_1 @7 (door, rail, turning light, alarm). ep1_citadel_03: console_actBusy ForceNPCToActBusy npc_elite_controltype_1 and _2 at console hints; Trigger_look_advisor (fieldofview .975, looktime 0.6) OnTrigger and OnTimeout > relay_enteringcorecontrol: AdvisorAlert @0, the elites Wake @0, assault Activate @0.13, relay_alarm1 @1, timer_alarm (8 s) Enable @1, Monitor_advisor_1 Disable @3. d3_breen_01: Breen pleads to it on Monitor_plead_console_1.
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): the Nexus or a Combine control room (docs/features/factions.md) could show an overseer's face on its screens for a few seconds at a time, cut in with static, and have Combine NPC staff at console spots who drop their work and fight when an intruder is seen for half a second. Map modules: console work spots (act busy hints) in Combine rooms and a screen surface named for the gamemode.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, combine, npc, logic, sound

- **See:** `valve_citadel/11_breen_office_cut.png`, `valve_citadel/20_e03_core_control.png`

### Defenders of the Citadel sleep until woken, and on a bridge each one's death wakes the next (`valve-wake-chain`)

Most of the Citadel's soldiers are placed asleep, waiting for a word, and are woken by what the player does (a door opened, a pipe broken, a look into a room) or by a comrade's death; on long bridges each defender's death wakes the next, so they come one at a time, in order, never all at once. Changing how our Combine defenders are spawned or woken: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep1_citadel_03: sleepstate 3 (waiting for an input) on 52 of 59 npc_combine_s; Wake from logic_relays (8), trigger_onces (8), comrades' OnDeath (6), func_physbox OnMotionEnabled (a ball pipe's door broken open, 4), path_track OnPass (1). ep1_citadel_02: 16 of 26 asleep; 13 OnDeath > Wake down the bridge squads (npc_elite_bridge2 > 2d > 2f, 2i > _3d...). Elites (combine_super_soldier) are 20 to 44% of the Citadel's soldiers (5 of 25 in d3_citadel_03, 12 of 58 in 04, 26 of 59 in ep1_citadel_03).
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): a raid on the Nexus (docs/features/city.md) could keep its inner defenders idle at posts until the raiders reach their stretch, and wake the next post when one falls, instead of all at once.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, combine, npc, logic, gameplay

- **See:** `valve_citadel/17_e02_bridge_over_the_drop.png`, `valve_citadel/18_e02_long_bridge.png`, `valve_citadel/20_e03_core_control.png`

### A place falling apart rumbles at random and its broken machines spark on their own (`valve-place-falling-apart`)

Once a place is breaking, let it say so by itself: every 12 to 49 seconds at random, a shake felt across the area with a far explosion and a scrape heard further still, and a second sound some time later, and, seen from afar, a chunk breaking off its top every 6 to 12 seconds; a story beat gets a harder, shorter shake. Each broken console sparks on its own random timer (every half a second to 2 seconds), switched on by whatever broke it and off a while after. No text. Fits our endings and the collapse, which are the owner's: ask the owner first before adding it to them.

- **Why:** ep1_citadel_01, 03, styleguide_destroyed_citadel: timer_rumble random 12 to 49 s (8 to 33 in ep1_citadel_02) > relay_rumble_random: shake_rumble_02 (amplitude 4, 4 s, frequency 120, radius 5000), citadel_ep01.ambient_explosion (radius 9000), a second sound @25. Story rumble relay_rumble_01: amplitude 12 for 1 s, then 4 for 4 s at 0.95, two sounds. 121 of the 15 maps' 209 logic_timers drive sparks, random 0.4 to 2 s, all start disabled and are Enabled by the relay that breaks the thing (d3_citadel_03 relay_consoleN_start_fx: explosion, sparks 2 s, timers on, stop at 10 s, the console's skin to 1). Seen from outside (ep1_citadel_00): timer_citadel_gibs (random 6 to 12 s) > case_citadel_gibs PickRandom over 5 env_shooters gib_citadel_chunk_1 to 5 (a chunk off the Citadel's top), timer_lightning (4 to 12 s, thunder @1 and @2). ep1_citadel_02b: small tremors every 6 to 12 s on the lift.
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): during the collapse or an ending's run-up (docs/features/collapse.md, endings.md), a city-wide random rumble every 12 to 49 s (screen shake 4 for 4 s, a far explosion sound); a Combine console or terminal that is wrecked sparks on a random 0.4 to 2 s timer for about 10 s.
- **Checked by:** map_lint 'wire': a spark timer starts disabled and something Disables or Kills it again; review
- **Tags:** valve, logic, sound, combine

- **See:** `valve_citadel/13_e00_citadel_foot.png`, `valve_citadel/25_style_destroyed_bridge.png`

### The Citadel shows its work in passing: workers busy at their task, a guard watching, there only while someone goes by (`valve-work-shown-in-passing`)

What the Combine does is shown, not told: stalkers at work with cables hanging to what they mend and sparks beside them, a soldier standing watching one, synths walking through a bay, one pod carrying a stalker past with a shriek. They appear just before someone comes past and are removed after. Fits a Combine quarter or Nexus as scenery; new NPC kinds there: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** d3_citadel_02: npc_stalker_busy1 to 3 on scripted_sequences (play working or idle2, flags 480 and 496), move_rope and keyframe_rope to their work, env_spark beside; npc_soldier_watchstalker3 beside busy3; spawned by point_templates when the player's pod passes pod_player12 (ForceSpawn @0 and @2) and Killed at pod_player2 and pod_player23; the gunship bay's stalker walks a 6-corner loop (aiscripted_schedule 4 s after the template spawns). d3_citadel_05: stalkerpod1 (a prop_dynamic stalker in a prisoner pod) at speed 70 over 1229, d3_citadel.stalker_shriek2 and breathing @2 at one node, shriek1 at the next; two crab synths walk path_gunshipbay_synths 1.5 s after the player's pod passes pod_player32 and are Killed at the path's end.
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): Combine scenery spots in the Nexus or Combine quarter, filled with a working NPC (a stalker at a panel with sparks, a guard idling beside) while a player is within reach and emptied after, as the life spots of valve-lift-diorama.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, combine, npc, logic, performance

- **See:** `valve_citadel/09_c05_synth_hall.png`, `valve_citadel/16_e01_stalker_gate_room.png`, `valve_citadel/22_e04_stalker_train_wreck.png`

### A great blast seen from afar is a light that keeps growing, a fog that turns white, then a fade (`valve-blast-grows`)

Show a huge explosion far off (the Citadel's) as a bright sprite whose size keeps multiplying by a small step many times a second, so it swells slowly and then fast, while the fog over everything lerps to white and the screen fades. No text, no countdown. For our endings, which are the owner's: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep1_intro: counter_explosion (math_counter, startvalue 8, max 999999) OutValue > sprite_end_final_explosion_1 SetScale; Timer_explosion (refire 0.05) > counter_explosion Multiply 1.0065, so about 14% a second (x1.14 a second, x49 in 30 s); Enabled 15 s after lrelay_alyx_vanish, Disabled when the G-Man's scene starts. relay_citadel_end: fog_controller_explo SetColorLerpTo 255 255 255 and SetEndDistLerpTo 1 with StartFogTransition @0.21, fade_explo_01 Fade, the camera's FOV blended from 1 to 80 over 2.7 s.
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): an ending that ends in a blast (docs/features/endings.md: the motherlode, the collapse) could grow a glow sprite at the blast's place by 14% a second and fade the fog to white before the fade to black, in place of words on screen.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, logic, lighting, story

### Each way in warns before its wave: a siren and lamps that show how big, 20 s ahead (`valve-wave-warning`)

Where attackers come from several holes or tunnels, each hole has its own looping siren and three lamps beside it. Before a wave, the holes it will use sound their siren and light one, two or three lamps for a small, middling or big wave; the attackers come out about 20 s later (15 s for the last wave); the siren stops when the first one is out and the lamps go out when that hole's attackers are all dead. Players learn where to stand and how bad it will be from the world alone, with no text. Changing how our nest or any defence announces its waves: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep2_outland_02, the mine arena: four tunnels, each with an ambient_generic siren (outland_ep02.cave_siren1 to 4_loop, radius 1250, spawnflags 16 or 17) and point_spotlights pathNN_light_easy, _medium, _hard whose lamp props change skin OnLightOn and OnLightOff. GO_spawnNN_easy, _medium, _hard and _final relays: siren PlaySound @0 (or 0.5), one to three lamps LightOn @0, the tunnel's npc_antlion_template_maker Enable @20 (@15 for _final). Each maker OnSpawnNPC > siren StopSound; OnAllSpawnedDead > that tunnel's lamps LightOff (@5 after the final wave). 16 makers: four tunnels by easy, medium, hard and final.
- **How:** Map modules: give each burrow or tunnel mouth of the nest (and any defended place with several ways in) a named siren and three named lamps (lc_nest_mouth_N_siren, _lamp_1 to 3). Gamemode (ask first): before a wave, sound the siren and light the lamps of the mouths it will use, and start the wave 20 s later. Ours (the owner, 21 November): the nest's alarm at the breach sounds when players open the emergency door, all three lamps, and the antlions come 11 s later, not 20 ("This is triggered by the players so they know what they are getting into already").
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, npc, logic, gameplay, sound

- **See:** `valve_ep2/05_o02_arena_holdout_cut.png`

### A wave fight grows by how many ways in are open, rests 6 s between waves, and stops itself if the room is overrun (`valve-wave-pattern`)

A defence against waves of creatures runs from one counter and a case: wave 1 uses one way in, then two, three, three, four, four, then all at once for the last; a wave ends when every open way's attackers are dead (the counter's target set to how many ways are open). Between waves: a 6 s rest, a save point, the NPC defenders say a line for that wave and are healed to full. Two safety valves: if 8 attackers are inside the defended room at once, the wave is cut short (no more come); corpses are capped at 3. Late in the fight the defence is taken down on purpose (the two turrets run dry at 50 and 60 s of wave 6), so the last waves feel desperate, and help arrives for the last one. When it ends, stragglers are killed off out of sight and the music fades in 7 s. Changing how our waves run: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep2_outland_02: caseHander_patternMaster (logic_case, 9 cases) fed by caseIncrementor (math_counter max 10): case 1 one medium tunnel, case 2 easy 3 and 2, case 3 easy 1, 4, 3, case 4 easy 3, 1 and medium 4, case 5 all four easy, case 6 easy 1, 3, 4 and medium 2, case 7 the four finals. mathCounter_antlionTracker_main SetHitMax 1, 2, 3, 3, 4, 4; each maker OnAllSpawnedDead adds 1; OnHitMax: autoSave, timer (logic_timer refire 6, disables itself) Enable @0.2, rebels SpeakResponseConcept TLK_outland_02_waveend, AddContext waveactive:0. caseIncrementor OutValue > npc_citizen SetHealth 100. trigger_antLionRoomTracker counts antlions in the room into mathCounter_antlionSurgeTracker (max 8) > end_antlion_wave (every maker SetMaxChildren 0, Disable). Case 4: ragdoll_manager SetMaxRagdollCount 3. Case 6: turret_buddy_1_disable_rl_1 @50 and _2 @60 (DepleteAmmo, env_steam, env_spark, the rebel's line), antlion relationships to the turrets changed @50. Final counter (max 4) OnHitMax: music FadeOut 6 @7, strays SetHealth 0 @8, vortigaunts SetReadinessLow. Makers: easy 3 or 4 alive of 16, one a second; medium 4 to 6 of 32 every 0.05 to 0.1 s; hard 4 of 40; final 2 or 3 alive of 21.
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): if the nest becomes a defence (or a raid on rebel ground is held), run waves by a table of which mouths open per wave (1, 2, 3, 3, 4, 4, all), end a wave when its attackers are dead, rest 6 s, and cut a wave short when 8 are inside the defended area at once.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, npc, logic, gameplay

- **See:** `valve_ep2/05_o02_arena_holdout_cut.png`

### When the fight is won, the attackers left over walk off to a far spot and vanish there, out of sight (`valve-swarm-retreats`)

A fight ends cleanly: when its boss or its last objective falls, the attackers still alive are not left to be mopped up. They are given one far target out of sight (an unseen bullseye they now hate most, or a walk-to spot), they run or walk there, and a trigger there removes each as it arrives. The players see the enemy break and leave. Good for a multiplayer server too: nothing is left wandering. Changing how our fights end: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep2_outland_05: guards_death_relay > retreat_relay: ai_relationship sand_vs_retreat and caves_vs_retreat (hate, rank 100) on retreat_bullseye (npc_bullseye far off, 1792 2816 480), antlion_caves and antlion_sand UpdateEnemyMemory retreat_bullseye, retreat_trigger1 (filter npc_antlion) OnStartTouch > !activator Kill @0.25. ep2_outland_10: siege_phase4_counter2 OnHitMax > siege_soldier_delete_sequence1 to 7 (scripted_sequences walking the soldiers away, each enabling a trigger that removes them). ep2_outland_12: victory_counter OnHitMax > victory_hunter_retreat_relay @7 and @9.
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): when a district fight, a raid or the nest's defence is won, send what is left of the losing side's NPCs to the nearest spawn point out of sight and remove them there, instead of letting them fight on.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, npc, logic, gameplay, performance

### Antlions in the open come from a pool that refills slowly: kill them all and you get a breather; thumpers make safe islands (`valve-antlion-pool`)

On open ground with antlions, keep only one or two out at a time, drawn from a small pool (3 to 5) that refills every 20 to 30 s, coming up at a random burrow. Killing them all buys a breather; lingering brings them back. Thumpers (512 reach) stand about 1400 to 1700 apart as safe islands to run between, and the way runs from one to the next. Our nest's makers have no end while its door is open: a pool would change that, so ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep2_outland_05: npc_antlion_template_maker antlion_maker_caves (maxlivechildren 1, pool_start 5, pool_max 5, pool_regen_amount 5, pool_regen_time 30, spawnflags 1040: random spawn node and fade corpse, frequency -1), antlion_maker_sand (2 alive, pool 3, refilled every 20 s), antlion_worker_maker_caves and _sand (1 alive, pool 2, 30 and 20 s). Three prop_thumper (combinethumper002, effectradius 512) at -80 1216, -752 2464, -80 4000: 1420 and 1680 apart. 35 burrow hints (hinttype 400) in the map. Valve's wiki: pool_max 0 turns the pool off; AddToPool, SetMaxPool, SetPoolRegenAmount and SetPoolRegenTime change it while running.
- **How:** Map: lc_nest_maker could carry pool_max 5, pool_regen_amount 5, pool_regen_time 30 (ask first). Gamemode: if the gamemode sends the nest's antlions itself, count a pool per nest that refills on a timer. Forest pieces: thumpers 512 reach, about 1400 to 1700 apart, if the owner wants antlions loose in a forest. HL2's coast (d2_coast_01, 03, 04): an env_player_surface_trigger (gamematerial 78, sand; Valve's wiki says it is meant for single player) Enables the antlion maker when the player steps on sand and Disables it 0 to 1 s after he steps off; the maker spawns round the player with two settings, on foot (pool regen 3 every 20 s, 4 alive, one every 0.2 s, radius 600) and in the car (regen 1 every 1 s, 3 alive, radius 700); point_antlion_repellant (radius 200 to 700) keep them off rocks, roads and docks; thumpers there reach 1000; d2_coast_11's sand crossing spawns 1000 round the player, 5 alive, one every 0.35 s.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, npc, logic, gameplay

- **See:** `valve_coast/13_coast11_sand_planks.png`

### A creature busy with its own thing until someone disturbs it: feeding, wandering to a lure, waiting to be seen (`valve-busy-until-disturbed`)

Not every creature waits to attack. Some are busy: antlions feeding on a body (it is their target, so they stay on it), workers walking to a lure, one on the ceiling that waits until a player looks at it. Hurting one breaks its interest; a step closer or a look sends them all at the players; after a while (15 to 16 s) it happens anyway. The player gets a moment to see the world before the fight. Changing how our nest's antlions or the forest's creatures behave: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep2_outland_01a: feasting_antlions_1 (three npc_antlion, startburrowed, eludedist 1024) with ai_relationship feasting_antlions_1_corpse_relationship (hate, rank 100) on feasting_antlions_1_corpse_bullseye (npc_bullseye, health 35000, OnDamaged > the corpse's phys explosion); func_clip_vphysics keeps the body in place; each antlion OnDamaged > !self SetRelationship the bullseye D_NU; feasting_antlions_corpse_relay > aiscripted_schedule (run to the player) @0.3, relationship reverted, bullseye and clip killed. lure_worker_1_trigger_5: workers Wake, lure_workers_bullseye relationship applied then killed @1. ceiling_spitter_aisc_1: ai_script_conditions (playeractorfov 45, true cone, timeout 15 to 16) > aiscripted_schedule. 161 ai_script_conditions in the 18 EP2 maps (48 in ep2_outland_12).
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): in the nest's first chamber, a few antlions feeding on a body until a player is within about 400 or shoots; in the forest, a creature that only acts when looked at.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, npc, logic, gameplay

### Antlion grubs lie in twos and threes along the floor and walls, 22 to 50 apart (`valve-grub-clusters`)

In a nest, grubs are not scattered evenly: most lie in small clusters (two or three, now and then up to six) within about 160, 22 to 50 apart inside a cluster, with a few alone; they sit on the cave's floor and walls along the way players walk, more of them where the nest is oldest. About 90 to 140 in a long cave, 8 in a small one. It would change our nest or our NPCs: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** 340 npc_antlion_grub in ep2_outland_01a (93), 02 (8), 03 (143), 04 (96). Nearest other grub: 01a median 50 (quartiles 36 and 116), 03 median 32 (26 and 41), 04 median 31 (25 and 70). Clusters within 160: 03 has 11 alone, 22 pairs, 21 threes, 5 fours, 1 five; 04 15 alone, 9 pairs, 14 threes, 1 four, 1 five, 2 sixes. A few named ones are squashed by the guardian in a scene (guard_squish_grub). Each OnDeathByPlayer counts toward an achievement. Valve's wiki: a grub glows faintly and drops a health nugget when killed.
- **How:** Map modules: NestTube places its grubs (19 now) in clusters of two or three, 22 to 50 apart, on the floor and the lower walls of the passage and chambers, a few alone; checked by the nearest-other spacing.
- **Checked by:** lint: in a nest piece, the median distance from each grub to its nearest other is 20 to 60, and at most a quarter stand alone (no other within 160)
- **Tags:** valve, props, npc, geometry

### A boss that cannot be beaten is escaped through gaps too small for it; it waits at the mouth and roars (`valve-boss-escape-gaps`)

A huge creature that chases players through tunnels is made unkillable and escaped, not fought: the way has low crawlspaces it cannot enter. While a player is inside one, it waits at the mouth, roaring and pounding, dust falling at random every 7 to 15 s; the moment the player comes out of either end it runs at him again. Its big moves (leaps, a wall bashed in) are staged, and it is heard down the tunnel (rubble, shakes, roars over 11 s) before it is seen. The owner asked for a hive boss in the nest's last chamber, separate from the infestation's boss: how it fights is the owner's to decide. It would change our nest or our NPCs: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep2_outland_04: npc_antlionguard maze_guard (cavernbreed 1, incavern 1, shovetargets bigcave_guard_rocks) OnHalfHealth > !self SetHealth 500. Five crawlspaces: maze_crawlspaceN_playerin_trigger > that crawlspace's hide_case relays (scripted_sequence at the mouth, post idle 'idle', a growl @0.5 to 1), maze_crawlspace_effects_timer (random 7 to 15 s > maze_effects_case PickRandomShuffle) Enable, the playerout triggers Enable; maze_crawlspaceN_playerout_triggers (one at each end) > the hide sequences CancelSequence, effects timer Disable, tunnels_maze_guard_getplayer_sched (aiscripted_schedule, goal !player, schedule 3: run) StartSchedule @0.1. guard_leap_ss_1 to 3 with stalactites dropped by relays. cave_guardtunnel_relay2 and 3: rubble, env_shake, impacts and roars @0, 1, 1.5, 3.4, 4.5, 8, 11 (relay3 @11 roar2). The way out: guard_exit_bash_board_1 to 7 (func_breakable health 1, each OnBreak spawning a broken board from a template).
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): if the hive boss is built, give the nest's last chamber two or three low crawlspaces (under 40 high) as refuges; the boss waits at a mouth with a roar while players are inside, and is heard down the passage before it shows.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, npc, logic, gameplay, sound

### An unseen wall that only the boss bumps into keeps it in its arena; leave the arena and it turns away (`valve-boss-fence`)

A big creature's fight stays where it belongs: a brush wall that blocks only that kind of creature (players and others pass) rings its arena, and it is leashed both ways: when the players run far out, it turns on someone else in the arena (or goes back to its spot) and forgets them; when they come back in, it hunts them again. A second boss comes about 15 s after the first, not together. For our hive boss and any big NPC: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep2_outland_05: guard_brushes, four func_brush with excludednpc npc_antlionguard and invert_exclusion 1 (block only antlion guards), StartDisabled, Enabled by spawn_guard2_rl. guard_retreat_trigger OnStartTouch: guard_1 UpdateEnemyMemory alyx, guard_2 UpdateEnemyMemory vort, lost_player relationships applied; guard_refresh_trigger: both UpdateEnemyMemory !player, relationships reverted; each guard OnLostPlayer and OnFoundPlayer apply and revert the same. guard_1_timer (refire 15, kills itself) > spawn_guard1_rl after guard 2. guards_death_counter max 2.
- **How:** Map modules: a hive boss's chamber gets a func_brush ring named lc_nest_boss_fence with excludednpc set to the boss's class and invert_exclusion 1. Gamemode (ask first): a boss loses interest in a player who leaves its chamber.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, npc, logic, gameplay, multiplayer

### A holdout that is a wait for a slow lift: call it, see it coming, fight until it arrives; it climbs only while someone rides (`valve-lift-holdout`)

Instead of a timer, players wait for a machine they can watch: the call button locks itself and lights up, the cage starts its long way down (about 35 s), a red lamp turns on, and 10 s after the call the attackers come. Riding up, the cage moves only while someone stands in it and goes back down to fetch anyone who stepped off. Fits house rule 9's exception (holding ground against attacks). A new kind of objective: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep2_outland_03: elevator_call_bottom (func_button, spawnflags 1025, wait -1) OnPressed: Lock, button model turn_On, elevator_1_turn_on_lights_rl, holdout_elevator_1 StartForward, holdout_antlions_respawn_trigger Enable, logic_start_redlight @5, the vortigaunt's line @5, spawn_end_zombies_rl @10, autosave. holdout_elevator_1: func_tracktrain startspeed 35, about 1260 to travel (about 36 s), move sound d3_breen.core_elevator_move1. holdout_elevator_1_playerriding_trigger_1 (parented to the cage): OnStartTouch StartBackward (up), OnEndTouch StartForward (back down), a nag timer while nobody rides. Zombie miners slumped on 21 work hints (hinttype 12, zombie_slump_a and _b) under ai_goal_actbusy (busysearchrange 64) wake as holdout_zombie_wakeup_rl deactivates the goals @0, 1, 3, 5, 7, antlions @5.
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): an objective where the players call a lift, cage or train in the mine or the bunker and hold until it arrives, the machine itself showing how long is left.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, npc, logic, gameplay

### The larval extract is a glowing thing carried from the nest, and vortigaunts heal with it in a ritual (`valve-extract-heals`)

The extract from the nest's last chamber is shown as a glowing prop with a light that moves with whoever carries it; vortigaunts use it to heal: it is handed over, eaten, and several vortigaunts stand round the hurt one, chanting, until they turn back to normal one after another a fraction of a second apart. Our nest already has lc_nest_extract waiting for a use: what it does is the owner's to decide (ask the owner first).

- **Why:** ep2_outland_02: ss_give_nectar (scripted_sequence nectar_give) OnScriptEvent01 > prop_extract2 SetParent vort_calvary_1 and SetParentAttachment nectar, extract_light_2 parented and TurnOn; SS_vort_eat_nectar OnScriptEvent01 > fx_nectar_juice (info_particle_system extract_vorteat_juice) at the mouth, sound_extract_juice, the extract and its light killed. lcs_vort_generator: chant loop @1, vort_singlechant_timer (random, lower bound 7 to 9). lcs_post_revive OnTrigger2 > four vortigaunts TurnBlue 0; OnTrigger1 > ss_vort_unfreeze, _vort1_, _vort2_, _vortc_ @0, 0.4, 0, 0.3. ep2_outland_04: vort_gather_essence_lcs, vort_chant_for_essence_ss, relay_extract_glows (9 lines) in the extract chamber.
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): lc_nest_extract could be carried out (glowing) and given to a freed vortigaunt in rebel territory, which then heals the people near it for a while, with a chant and the vortigaunts' glow.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, npc, logic, gameplay, story

### Hunters come in twos and threes, are glimpsed one at a time first, pin the hiding place, and the next comes when one is half dead (`valve-hunters-in-squads`)

Hunters are never alone: two or three in one squad (their own AI flanks), each kept to a zone of the fight by a hint group. Their arrival is shown before it is fought: each is glimpsed in turn (1.5 and 2.5 s apart, when a player looks), and while players hide they fire at spots by the hiding place to pin them, not to kill. The next hunter comes when the current one is at half health, not on a timer. Hunters need Episode Two's content; adding them to our Combine is the owner's choice (ask first). It would change our nest or our NPCs: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** 27 npc_hunter in 6 EP2 maps: squads ep2_outland_06a hunter_squad (3), 10 hunter_squad (2), 11a huntersquadgo (3), 12 one squad of three per strider (npc_hunter_maker SpawnMultiple 3 then FollowStrider). hintgroup with hintlimiting 1: 06a hunter_zone, 10 hunter_1_hintgroup1, 11a huntercatwalk. 06a: sc_stalker4 (ai_script_conditions, FOV 60, timeout 3) > stalker_2_looktrigger @1.5, stalker_1_looktrigger @2.5; bullseye_hunter1 to 5 and bullseye_player_hiding by the hiding place, killed by trigger_release_hunters and relay_shooter_withdraw; hunter_1 damagefilter filter_invuln until then; hunter_death_counter (max 3) > music FadeOut 6 @1, the next task @4. 11a: hunter and hunter_4 OnHalfHealth > relay_template_hunter1 (the next hunter, manhack makers, once); hunter_4 crawls along path_hunter_crawlshoot then SetSquad huntersquadgo.
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): if the Combine gets hunters (an Overwatch response, the forest's patrol), spawn them two or three to a squad, show each before it attacks, and bring a replacement when one is at half health.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, npc, combine, logic, gameplay

### A house under siege is safe inside: the attackers shoot its windows in turns, punish whoever steps out, and break in only at the end (`valve-siege-house`)

When the Combine besiege a building the players hold, the siege runs in phases (by time, about 80 and 55 s, then by kills): soldiers fire at the windows in sets that change every 6 to 8 s, keeping heads down; hunters hide round the building and fire in; the hunters ignore a player who stays inside, but go for one who steps out and forget him when he is back in. In the last phase each hunter goes to a door, plants itself and bursts it (the door made a target only it can hurt). A new kind of raid on rebel ground: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep2_outland_10: siege_phase1_relay: window bullseye relays @5, roof soldiers, hunter makers Enable @7, hunter neutral-to-player relationships @0.5, hunter_hates_outside_player_trigger Enable @7.5 (OnStartTouch reverts hunter 2's neutral relationship and UpdateEnemyMemory !player; OnEndTouch applies it again; wait 4), siege_phase2_relay @80; phase 2: the second assault line, phase3 @55; phase 4 by logic_branch_listener (both branches true); phase 5: hunter_1_killdoor_relay and hunter_2_ (ai_changehintgroup release, UseSiegeTargets, hunter_1_breakin_seq: plant, inn_basement_door_bullseye SetDamageFilter filter_hunter_1, the hunter hates the door bullseye). inn_front_windows_bullseyes_relay: bullseyes front 2, 4, 6 hated @0 to 6, front 1, 2, 3 @8 to 15, again @17 (spawnflags 2: fires again).
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): a Combine siege of a rebel safehouse in phases; the soldiers aim at its windows in turn; anyone who steps out is hunted; the breach comes last, through a door.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, npc, combine, logic, gameplay

### A supply point holds one thing and makes another 7 s after it is taken: it never runs out and never piles up (`valve-supply-pad`)

Where a fight needs a steady supply of one tool (a striderbuster, a heavy weapon, a medkit), put it on a pad: one sits there; when it leaves the pad, the pad hums and spins up and 7 s later a new one appears. Never more than one per pad, so nothing piles up on a busy server; the pad hums so it can be found and is marked on the car's radar. Changing how the Resistance's supplies work: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep2_outland_12: six weapon_striderbuster on Magnusson teleporter pads (teleporter_3 to 8, prop_dynamic magnusson_teleporter, idle hum radius 3000). teleporter_N_trigger (trigger_multiple, spawnflags 8: physics objects, wait 7) OnEndTouchAll > the pad SetAnimation spinup, the trigger Disable, teleporter_N_magdevice_template ForceSpawn @7; the template OnEntitySpawned > the trigger Enable. info_radar_target teleporter_N_radar_target (type 1, radius -1) by each. Each device OnShotDown (shattered by a hunter) > mag_shotdown_counter, the player says TLK_HUNTERSHOTMAG and outpost_citizen_hunterhint_conditions Enable (a rebel says to shoot the hunters first).
- **How:** Map modules: an lc_supply_pad piece (a pad prop, a trigger feeling physics objects, a point_template of the item) for the Resistance base and the bunker. Gamemode (ask first): which item each pad gives.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, logic, gameplay, multiplayer, props

### A big defence has a clear win and a clear loss in the world, and loses its outposts one by one, people first (`valve-defence-win-loss`)

A large defence (waves of striders and their hunters from three sides) ends in a way everyone sees: it is won when the last few big attackers are dead (gates open, people walk out, an announcer), and lost if one reaches the heart (an alarm and a warning when one gets close, it slows there so players can still stop it). On the way, outlying buildings are lost one at a time: hunters drive the people out (they run on set paths about 20 s after the walker appears), then the walker shells the building and it swaps whole for its ruin. Defenders who fall are replaced one at a time from the base's own door. Changing how our district fights or the Resistance base's defence end: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep2_outland_12: 13 npc_strider from npc_template_makers north, east and west, each relay (strider_7_west_relay: maker Spawn @10, strider_west_hunter_maker SpawnMultiple 3 @10, FollowStrider @11, lodge_evacuate_seq1 to 7 @20, lodge_hunter_bullseyes_relay @11, a 30 s cycle of hunter bullseyes in the building); cabin_destroy_conditionsmet_relay: strider SetCannonTarget cabin_strider_bullseye; cabin_destroy_relay (25 lines: brushes, lods and props Kill, breakables Break, physics EnableMotion, sparks to 20 and 30 s, smoke @1, broken model templates). victory_counter (max 5) fed by the last five striders' OnDeath: base gate and doors Open, victory scene @5, hunter retreat @7 and @9, defenders walk back @18. strider_closetobase_trigger (filter_strider): alarm, announcer @1.5, ScaleGroundSpeed 0.75; OnEndTouchAll stops the alarm. base_destroy_relay: shockwave, white fade, failure text @6, Reload @8. defence_cit_ar2_maker1 and _rpg_ (one alive, every 5 s, enabled from the start); lodge, cabin and outpost citizen makers open their base door on spawn.
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): a district fight or a Combine assault on the Resistance base gets a win (the attackers' last wave dead) and a loss (an attacker reaches the base's flag or its heart), told by alarms and the place itself; outlying rebel posts are evacuated before they fall.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, npc, logic, gameplay, combine

### A big blast door takes 8 to 27 s, with a lock that moves first and its own start, loop and end sounds (`valve-big-door-slow`)

A huge door is slow and loud: its locking bar moves first (and shuts again after it closes), then the leaves travel for 8 to 27 s with a start sound, a loop while moving and an end sound; an alarm or rotating lights run while it moves. Our bunker's sliding leaves take 4.6 s (the owner asked for 'somewhat slowly' with 'a big hoo-ha'): slowing them is the owner's call, so ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep2_outland_11: doors_biground_1, two func_door_rotating leaves, distance 60 at speed 6 (10 s), noise1 ep2_outland_11.blastdoors_open_loop, noise2 blastdoors_open_end, start sound sound_doors_biground_1, OnFullyClosed > doorlock_biground_1 (func_door, speed 100) Close and blastdoors_close_end; opened by path_elevator_1a1 OnPass (the lift passing). ep2_outland_11a: silo_seal_door and _2, func_movelinear movedistance 136 at speed 5 (27 s), startsound launchdoors_close_start, sound_launchdoors_loop, sound_launchdoors_stop @26, Alarm_relay1 while closing; doors_biground_2 speed 10. ep2_outland_12: base_gate_door, func_movelinear 240 at speed 30 (8 s), sound ep2_outland_12.base_gate. Ours: BunkerForest.door leaves 120 at 26 (4.6 s).
- **How:** Map: if the owner agrees, the bunker door's leaves could move at about 10 (12 s), with a lock bar, a start, loop and end sound and the red lamps turning while they move.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, doors, logic, sound

- **See:** `valve_ep2/25_o11a_base_halls_cut.png`, `valve_ep2/26_o11a_silo2_section.png`

### A broken door jams and jerks open and shut at random, with sparks and clicks, until someone repairs it (`valve-jammed-door`)

A door that has broken down is shown broken: it jerks open and shut every 0.8 to 1.5 s, each time with one of several clicks and a spark, until someone mends it; then it closes slowly and locks. It is a job waiting to be done, told by the door itself. A repair job on it would be a minigame (house rule 9), never a stand-and-wait. A new job: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep2_outland_11: door_silo_lab_outside_1_malfunction_timer (logic_timer random 0.8 to 1.5) > the door Toggle, the reset relay Enable; the door (func_door, speed 80) OnOpen, OnFullyOpen, OnClose, OnFullyClosed > malfunction_case PickRandomShuffle (five ambient_generic button sounds, Buttons.snd41 to 47, flags 48), OnFullyOpen > env_spark SparkOnce. The reset relay: Close @0.3, timer Disable, SetSpeed 5 @0.2, the silo door model SetAnimation lock @0.6, a sound. Summons a vortigaunt to fix it (summon_uriah_rl, once).
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): a 'fix the jammed door' job for Combine engineers or the Resistance, the door jerking until the wiring minigame is done.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, doors, logic, gameplay, sound

### An advisor is felt before it is seen: a pulse every 8 to 12 s that everyone near flinches at (`valve-advisor-pulse`)

The Combine's masters are a presence: where one is kept, a pulse comes at random every 8 to 12 s (a sound, a brief blur on the screen and a short colour flash) and the people near react to it, one after another; after three it comes less often (14 to 20 s). When it wakes, steam bursts in a quick run, the player is slowed hard and it strikes about 8 s later. No flickering lights (the owner): the pulse is sound and a screen effect. Anything with advisors in Living City: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep2_outland_07: pod_pulse_timer (random 8 to 12) > pod_pulse sound, pulse_overlay Start @0.1 and Stop @0.5, pod_pulse_jitter StartEffect 2 @0.08, colorcorrection_flash Enable @0.01 and Disable @2, pulse_react_relay_1 to 3 (each a reaction scene @1.5, enabling the next, once); pulse_react_relay_3 > timer bounds 14 and 20. relay_alarm: steam bursts @1, 1.6, 2.6, 2.9, 3.3, 3.5, speedmod_player ModifySpeed 0.1 @3, Advisor_Attack @7.9 (zoom, fade, overlay). ep2_outland_11a relay_adv_pulse and 12a relay_adv_pulse_long: the same pulse. 12a relay.advisor.scene: ModifySpeed 0.1, shakes @2 and @3, far lights killed @1, 3.7 and 5. Not to copy: timer_pod_flicker_slow, _med, _fast (the pod's lights blinking).
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): if an advisor ever appears (the Nexus, an ending), announce it by a pulse with a sound and a short screen effect, and let nearby NPCs flinch.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, npc, combine, logic, sound

### Against an aircraft, cover does not last: every 20 s one shelter is picked and bombed apart (`valve-cover-wrecked`)

In a long fight against a gunship or helicopter, the places players hide in are taken away one at a time: every 20 s one shelter is picked at random and bombed, and it breaks apart in one go (its pieces freed, a burst, fire, a scorch mark), with a danger sound first so NPCs clear out. Hits on the aircraft bring a bigger bomb; at half health it makes a last effort. Players must keep moving instead of camping one spot. Combine air support in our fights: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep2_outland_08: timer_crate_activate (logic_timer refire 20) > case_destruction PickRandomShuffle; trigger_bomb_outside_shack (filtered, wait 1.5) > chopper DropBombAtTargetAlways target_bomb_11; relay_healthshack_1 (19 lines: physboxes EnableMotion, break_healthshack_1 Break @0.3, propane Break @0.2, explosions @0.1, a gib shooter, a decal, fire, brushes swapped, aisound_explosion_1 EmitAISound @0.3); relay_exploding_tower (30 lines), relay_exploding_shack (12). npc_helicopter chopper: OnDamaged > counter_megabomb (max 3) > relay_megabomb; OnHalfHealth > relay_megabomb (once); OnShotDown > crash alarm, cheers @1, save.
- **How:** Gamemode (ask first): if a gunship ever joins a district fight, give it a list of the district's shelters and let it wreck one every 20 s, each shelter a swap set (valve-swap-broken).
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** valve, npc, combine, logic, gameplay

### A forest is trees standing apart in bands along the way, with open ground between (`valve-forest-layout`)

Plant Episode Two's woods thin and uneven: most trees alone (no other within 160), the nearest neighbour 150 to 300 away, 2 to 10 trees in a square 1000 a side; few on the path's edge (within 128 of the walk), the thickest band 256 to 1024 off it, thinning beyond 1024 where cards take over; and leave about half of the 512 squares with no tree at all. A few small clumps of three to five, never an even scatter. Our forests were planted and thinned at the owner's asking: ask the owner first before changing how thick they stand.

- **Why:** Ten EP2 forest maps (21 November): nearest other tree, median 148 (ep2_outland_05), 154 (01), 165 (10a), 206 (10), 211 (12a), 244 (12), 252 (09), 261 (06a), 294 (07), 298 (08). Alone within 160: 54 to 97% (06a 85%, 07 97%, 12 81%); only 01, 05 and 10a have a quarter in groups of three or more. Trees per 1000x1000 by distance from the AI nodes: 0-128 0.1 to 7, 128-256 0.5 to 8, 256-512 2.2 to 10.6, 512-1024 1.8 to 6.4, beyond 1024 0 to 3. Of 512 squares with ground in the trees' box, 44 to 82% hold no tree (10a 0.44, 12 0.47, 10 0.48, 01 0.57, 06a 0.59, 07 0.73). Ours (map_forest.py Forest.plant, a 150 grid kept at 0.23 to 0.48, 0.64 on the ridge): about 14 a 1000 square over the valley (21 before the pond and paths), 28 on the ridge band, median gap 132, 27% alone.
- **How:** map_forest.py: plant by distance from the trail and the gate's way in (bands as above) rather than an even grid; a Poisson-disc spacing of about 150 with a gap test, a clump now and then (three to five within 120); leave whole 512 squares open; keep the ridge's own band for the cards (valve-forest-border). Count and print alone share, median gap and empty squares with each build.
- **Checked by:** proposed: map_forest prints the median gap between trees (want 150 to 300), the share alone within 160 (want over a half) and the share of 512 squares with no tree (want 0.4 to 0.8)
- **Tags:** valve, forest, layout, props

- **See:** `valve_ep2/01_o01_forest_from_above.png`, `valve_ep2/02_o01_forest_eye.png`, `valve_ep2/11_o05_wood_and_wall_from_above.png`, `valve_ep2/13_o06a_forest_bowl_overview.png`, `valve_ep2/15_o07_farm_in_woods_from_above.png`, `valve_ep2/17_o08_wooded_slope_by_rail_from_above.png`, `valve_ep2/18_o09_woods_by_the_yard_from_above.png`, `valve_ep2/19_o10_forest_valley_overview.png`, `valve_ep2/20_o10_forest_road_eye.png`, `valve_ep2/21_o10a_thick_wood_from_above.png`, `valve_ep2/22_o10a_thick_wood_eye.png`, `valve_ep2/28_o12_battle_woods_overview.png`, `valve_ep2/32_sg_forest_room_overview.png`

### Trees sink into the ground, deeper on a slope, and some lean (`valve-tree-footing`)

Set every tree's origin below the ground under it: 5 to 13 on level ground, 20 to 50 on a slope, so no root shows over the downhill side. Lean a tenth to a half of the trees 3 to 11 degrees (a few to 25), more on slopes and at the forest's edge, never a cut tree upright on a slope. Turn them on 15 degree steps or freely. Trees are solid static props that cast shadows and fade out between about 4200 and 7000. Pieces already built keep their look: ask the owner first before changing them.

- **Why:** EP2 (21 November), origin minus the displaced ground under it: median -4.7 (01), -9.2 (05), -9.6 (06a), -10.5 (07), -6.6 (08), -9.6 (09), -11.9 (10), -10.4 (10a), -6.4 (12), -13.3 (12a); the deepest tenth -20 to -54. Leaning more than 2 degrees: 01 43%, 05 61%, 06 57%, 08 23%, 12 20%, 10a 15%, 06a 14%, 10 10%, 07 7%, 09 0%; median lean 4 to 11, 90th 10 to 27. Yaw on 15 degree steps 53 to 99%. fademaxdist: 07 7000 on 296 of 313, 01 5000 to 5200, 10a 2200 on 164 small ones and 4200 to 5200 on the rest; disableshadows 0 on every tree; solid 6. Ours: 8 down everywhere, no lean, yaw on 5, fade 2800 to 3400.
- **How:** map_forest.py Forest.static for trees: sink = 6 + 0.25 x the ground's drop across the trunk (from the height function at 32 each side), lean = 3 to 9 degrees for one tree in five (one in three on slopes over 0.4), leaning downhill; fade (4200, 6000).
- **Checked by:** proposed: map_forest checks every tree is at least 4 below the lowest of five ground heights under its trunk
- **Tags:** valve, forest, props, geometry

- **See:** `valve_ep2/01_o01_forest_from_above.png`, `valve_ep2/11_o05_wood_and_wall_from_above.png`, `valve_ep2/29_o12_forest_edge_eye.png`

### A forest's edge is cards past the last trees, a clip on the slope and painted hills in the sky (`valve-forest-border`)

Stop players in the woods without a wall they can see: an invisible player clip laid along the top of a slope or among the last trees (tall, 200 to 600 long, turned with the ground); past the last real trees (about 1000 to 2000 from the walk) flat tree cards (tree_pine_card_cluster_01/02, tree_deciduous_card_01, tree_pine_tall_cluster_01), not solid; and the far distance painted on brushes in the 3D skybox (mountainrange materials), with no props there at all. Our forests are held by a ridge 440 high and planted thickest on it: ask the owner first before trading the ridge's trees for cards.

- **Why:** EP2 (21 November): cards 3 to 42 a map (12: 42, 09: 27, 07: 22, 10a: 21), median 870 to 2340 from the AI nodes, 500 to 1200 beyond the nearest real tree, solid 0 on 80 to 100% in most maps; in 12 no real tree stands more than 512 from the walk, cards stand beyond. toolsplayerclip brushes: 26 to 177 a forest map, median 54 to 570 from the nodes, height median 336 to 896, length median 192 to 576, half off the axes. Steep displacement 0.13 to 1.43 of the flat area. The 3D skybox of 01, 05 to 10 and 12a holds no tree prop: brushes with mountainrange_far_00c/02c/04c, mountains_skybox02, groundplane_01c (8 to 44 brushes, 0 to 11 displacements). ep2_outland_01 paints trees onto its cliff displacements with clifftrees_cardboard01a and 02a (1,562 of displaced area beside cliff03a/04a).
- **How:** map_forest.py: a ring of card props on the ridge's far side, 1000 to 1500 from the trail, solid 0; a player clip along the ridge top; keep real trees inside it. The skybox is the city's (map_merge): no change.
- **Checked by:** proposed: map_forest lists card props within 600 of the trail (they show their flatness up close)
- **Tags:** valve, forest, layout, performance

- **See:** `valve_ep2/01_o01_forest_from_above.png`, `valve_ep2/11_o05_wood_and_wall_from_above.png`, `valve_ep2/13_o06a_forest_bowl_overview.png`, `valve_ep2/15_o07_farm_in_woods_from_above.png`, `valve_ep2/16_o07_farmyard_edge_eye.png`, `valve_ep2/17_o08_wooded_slope_by_rail_from_above.png`, `valve_ep2/18_o09_woods_by_the_yard_from_above.png`, `valve_ep2/19_o10_forest_valley_overview.png`, `valve_ep2/28_o12_battle_woods_overview.png`, `valve_ep2/29_o12_forest_edge_eye.png`, `valve_ep2/32_sg_forest_room_overview.png`

### The forest floor: ferns along the path, rocks and logs tilted, grass from the ground's own material (`valve-forest-floor`)

Ground cover comes from the ground material's detail sprites, switched off (a no-sprite twin of the material) where people walk; fern props are few (a tenth to a half of the tree count) and line the path's edges, not the tree feet; rocks lie tilted (four in ten to three in four more than 5 degrees) and part sunk, 150 to 350 from the nearest tree; logs and fallen trees lie tilted, stumps near the trees. Nothing small is solid. Pieces already built keep their look: ask the owner first before changing them.

- **Why:** EP2 (21 November): every map sets detailmaterial detail/detailsprites and detailvbsp detail.vbsp; walked ground in blendgrassdirt01_noprop (07: 14,763 against 6,246 by area for blendgrassdirt01; 10a 6,574 against 1,099). Plants (fern01 nearly always, fern01lg, grass_cluster03a, shrub_01a) 6 to 155 a map, 0.04 to 0.5 per tree, solid 0 on all, median 70 to 370 from the nodes and 110 to 500 from a tree. Rocks 17 to 174 a map (rockcluster01/02, rock_coast02a to h, rocks_large*_veg, forestrock_cluster01/02), tilted over 5 degrees 41 to 76%, median 160 to 340 from a tree. Logs and stumps 2 to 133 (tree_stump01, fallentree01/02, fallentree_dry01/02, forest_log01), tilted 39 to 100%. Ours: 170 plants a forest (0.74 per tree) anywhere not steep, 34 rocks and 12 logs, none tilted.
- **How:** map_forest.py: plants mostly within 64 to 256 of the trail; rocks pitch and roll 5 to 25 degrees for two in three, sunk 10 to 20; logs rolled to the slope. Check the ground material has detail sprites, and use a no-sprite twin on the trail if one exists (ask before adding materials).
- **Checked by:** proposed: map_forest prints the share of rocks tilted (want 0.4 to 0.75) and of plants within 256 of the trail
- **Tags:** valve, forest, props

### Rail fences of the woods run end to end along the road and trail (`valve-forest-fences`)

Mark a forest road or trail with Episode Two's rail fences (props_forest/fence_border_128/256/512 and fence_trail_128/256/512), each piece set end to end with the last, 150 to 260 off the walk, turned freely to follow the way, border fences upright, trail fences sometimes leaning a few degrees; keep chain-link to the bases and yards. Pieces already built keep their look: ask the owner first before changing them.

Chain-link exterior_fence002* stands at the yards and bases (09: 42 of 118 fence props).

### Antlion caves: tunnels about 250 wide and 200 high, opening now and then into chambers (`valve-cave-proportions`)

Make a natural cave as about three parts tunnel to one part chamber: tunnels 220 to 300 across at the narrowest (a tenth down to 120 to 180), 160 to 240 high (a quarter over 320); chambers 512 or more across and 1000 to 1800 long. Every wall, roof and floor displaced. Our cave and nest are built and walked: ask the owner first before changing their size.

- **Why:** Rays from every AI node in ep2_outland_01a, 02, 03, 04 (21 November), nodes with a displaced roof and walls (297, 40, 287, 281): narrowest width median 298, 131, 266, 219; tenth 117 to 180; height median 243, 148, 243, 220, quartiles 158 to 190 and 319 to 371. In tunnels under 384 wide: 60 to 74%; in chambers 512 or more: 17 to 20%; the longest way across, 75th percentile 1500 to 1840. Ours: the cave hung on a square 320 by 320 (CaveForest.HALF, TALL); the nest 345 wide in its narrows and up to 760 in its chambers (NestTube radius 150 to 330), chambers about a third of its length.
- **How:** map_forest.py: if the owner wants it, narrow the cave's tunnels to about 260 between the mouth and the chamber and lower them to about 220; NestTube: radius about 120 in the narrows.
- **Checked by:** proposed: map_forest casts rays from the cave's path nodes and prints narrowest width and height quartiles

### Antlion grubs lie in twos to fours on the walls, each group with a small yellow light (`valve-grub-groups`)

Place npc_antlion_grub in groups of two to four, 20 to 50 apart, the groups 100 to 200 apart; half on the walls (turned to lie on them), a few on the ceiling, a quarter within 60 of the walk; put one small light in each group, about 30 to 40 from it: colour 255 245 157, brightness 30 to 80, quadratic. Our nest has its grubs one at a time: ask the owner first.

- **Why:** ep2_outland_01a (93 grubs), 03 (143), 04 (96), styleguide_caves (100) (21 November): nearest grub median 23 to 51; groups (linked within 128) mostly of 2 to 4 (03: 23 pairs, 20 threes; 01a: 19 pairs, 8 threes); on walls (pitch or roll 45 to 135) 32 to 63, on ceilings 0 to 13; distance to AI node quartile 59 to 140. Lights within 160 of a grub: 43 (01a), 60 (03), 47 (04), 18 of 24 (style room); median 30 to 43 from the grub in 01a and the style room; colour 255 245 157 on 38 of 43 (01a), 57 of 60 (03); brightness 30 to 80; spawnflags 4 on most. Ours (map_forest.py NestTube): 19 grubs one every 110 along the wall feet, upright, six lights for the whole nest.
- **How:** map_forest.py NestTube: groups of 2 to 4 round a point on the wall (theta 0.3 to 1.2 from the floor), each grub turned to the wall's normal, one light 255 245 157 50 beside each group.

### Caves are lit by three kinds of small light, never far apart (`valve-cave-light`)

Light a cave with many small steady lights of three kinds: the grubs' pale yellow (255 245 157, 30 to 80), the miners' cage lamps (255 226 174, 80 to 140, on a lit lamp model) along the works, and a cold dim fill (132 158 202 at about 25) where neither reaches; so that no walking spot is more than about 450 to 600 from a light, half within 180. Fog dark blue (18 23 35) out to 2800. Pieces already built keep their look: ask the owner first before changing them.
